  test('TC08: permanent delete of versioned file reclaims all quota', async () => {
    test.slow();

    const fileName = `bin-versioned-${runId}.txt`;

Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the findings:

## Summary: Soft-Delete Flow Analysis

Based on my code review, here are the answers to your questions:

**1. Optimistic vs. After-Operation UI Removal:**
The file list item is **NOT removed optimistically**. It's removed only **after** the delete operation completes and the SDK emits a `folder:updated` event. The flow is:
- User confirms delete → `handleDeleteConfirm()` (line 509-518 useFileBrowserActions.ts) → awaits `deleteItem()` 
- `deleteItem()` awaits the full SDK operation (deleteToBin or deleteItem)
- SDK publishes IPNS changes
- SDK emits `folder:updated` event
- `useFolderStore.subscribeToSdk()` (line 206-229 folder.store.ts) receives event and calls `updateFolderChildren()` 
- Children array in folder state is updated, triggering list re-render
- Only then does the item disappear from UI

**2. Code Path Trace:**
- Context menu delete: `ContextMenu.tsx:184` (handleDelete) → calls `onDelete()`
- Handler: `useFileBrowserActions.ts:389-391` (handleDeleteClick) → opens confirm dialog
- Confirmation: `useFileBrowserActions.ts:509-518` (handleDeleteConfirm) → awaits `deleteItem(parentId)`
- Mutation: `useFolderMutations.ts:338-376` (handleDelete) → calls `deleteWithBehavior()` (line 17-38) → SDK `deleteToBin()` or `deleteItem()`
- Store update: SDK event → `folder.store.ts:206-229` (subscribeToSdk) → `updateFolderChildren()` (line 92-103)

**3. Replaced Files:**
Deleting a replaced file follows the **same code path**. The `ReplaceFileDialog.tsx` creates a new version via `updateFile()` (line 41-49), which updates metadata with `fileId` and publishes. The file ID remains the same in the folder's children, so deletion uses the same `item.id` identifier. No versioning affects deletion logic.

**4. IPNS Publishing & Error Handling:**
Yes, deletion **awaits** the IPNS publish (via SDK's `deleteToBin()` or `deleteItem()`). If the operation fails, an exception is caught at line 515-516, logged, and **silently swallowed** — the dialog closes regardless (`closeConfirmDialog()` at line 514), but the item remains visible since no `folder:updated` event is emitted. The error is only logged to console.

**File references:**
- useFolderActions.ts:509-518 (delete confirmation & error handling)
- useFolderMutations.ts:338-376 (delete mutation with IPNS publish await)
- useFolderMutations.ts:17-38 (deleteToBin vs deleteItem choice)
- folder.store.ts:206-229 (SDK event subscription for state update)
```

> AGENT

This is the decisive finding. The soft-delete is **not optimistic** — the item only disappears after the SDK's `deleteToBin()` (which includes an **IPNS publish**) completes and emits `folder:updated`. And per the agent, a thrown delete is caught, logged, and **silently swallowed** — the dialog closes but the item stays visible. That exactly matches the symptom (item stays visible *and still selected*).

> AGENT

Confirmed the silent-swallow at `useFileBrowserActions.ts:512-517`: on a thrown delete, the error is only `logger.error`'d — nothing surfaces, and the list item is never removed (no `folder:updated` event fires). That's exactly the symptom in the trace.

## Verdict: real and intermittent — not a clean flake. And it's in code this exact push changed.

**What actually failed:** `recycle-bin.spec.ts:377 › TC08` at line 415 — the **soft-delete** step (not the permanent delete). After right-click → Delete → Confirm, the file `bin-versioned-…txt` never left the main file list; the locator stayed *visible and still `--selected`* for the full 30s. 179/180 passed.

**Why it's not "just a flaky test":**

1. **The failing path is exactly what commit `1abceb4b8` (#488) rewrote.** Web soft-delete is *not* optimistic — the item only disappears after `deleteToBin()` → **IPNS publish** → `folder:updated`. #488 changed `useFileOperations.ts` plus the SDK publish/409-merge core (`file/index.ts`, `folder/index.ts`, new `folder/merge.ts`, `client.ts`). The delete depends on that path.
2. **TC08 is uniquely positioned to hit the new 409-merge code.** It uploads v1, *replaces* with v2, then deletes — three IPNS publishes to the same folder in quick succession. Rapid back-to-back publishes are precisely what produces 409 write-conflicts (stale sequence), i.e. the brand-new merge branch. The basic-delete test (TC01) and every other delete/move/quota test passed — they don't do the replace-then-delete sequence.
3. **The symptom matches a swallowed/stalled delete, not a UI race.** The delete either threw (caught + only logged at `useFileBrowserActions.ts:515`) or hung in 409 retry past 30s. Note `test.slow()` does *not* extend the explicit `{ timeout: 30000 }` on `waitForItemToDisappear`, so a slower new code path can blow it on its own.

**Contrast with the genuinely-flaky one:** the prior main failure (`158addcca`) was infra — `wallet-login-button` never rendered, cascading 18 tests. Totally different signature. This one is a single, specific failure in freshly-changed code.

**Two real issues worth fixing regardless of re-run outcome:**
- The new 409-merge/publish path may not reliably complete a delete under rapid successive publishes to the same IPNS name (`folder/merge.ts` + `client.ts`).
- The web UI **silently swallows delete failures** — `useFileBrowserActions.ts:515-516` only `console.error`s. A failed delete leaves the item with zero user-visible feedback. That's a correctness/UX gap independent of the flake.

**Recommendation:** Don't just re-run and move on. I'd (a) re-run the Web E2E job once for a reproducibility data point — we only have one run containing #488's code — and (b) inspect the new `folder/merge.ts`/`client.ts` 409 path for the replace→delete sequence.

1	import { type FolderChild } from '@cipherbox/core';
2	
3	/**
4	 * Three-way merge of FolderChild arrays.
5	 *
6	 * Implements the D-01/D-02 merge semantics:
7	 *
8	 * - local-add (in local, not base, not remote): kept
9	 * - remote-add (in remote, not base, not local): kept
10	 * - added-by-both (in local AND remote, not base): last-write-wins by modifiedAt (>= prefers local)
11	 * - local-delete, remote-unchanged: dropped
12	 * - local-delete, remote-edited-after-base: edit-beats-delete, remote version kept
13	 * - remote-delete, local-kept: local version kept
14	 * - modified-in-both: last-write-wins by modifiedAt (>= prefers local on tie)
15	 * - empty/undefined base (D-02 fallback): union of local ∪ remote keyed by id, no deletions
16	 *
17	 * All children are keyed by `c.id` (UUID — stable identity).
18	 * Missing/undefined modifiedAt is treated as 0 for ordering.
19	 * Input arrays are not mutated.
20	 */
21	export function mergeChildren(
22	  base: FolderChild[],
23	  local: FolderChild[],
24	  remote: FolderChild[]
25	): FolderChild[] {
26	  const baseById = new Map(base.map((c) => [c.id, c]));
27	  const localById = new Map(local.map((c) => [c.id, c]));
28	  const remoteById = new Map(remote.map((c) => [c.id, c]));
29	
30	  const allIds = new Set([...localById.keys(), ...remoteById.keys()]);
31	  const merged: FolderChild[] = [];
32	
33	  for (const id of allIds) {
34	    const b = baseById.get(id);
35	    const l = localById.get(id);
36	    const r = remoteById.get(id);
37	
38	    if (l && !r && !b) {
39	      // local-add
40	      merged.push(l);
41	    } else if (!l && r && !b) {
42	      // remote-add
43	      merged.push(r);
44	    } else if (l && r && !b) {
45	      // added-by-both: last-write-wins, >= prefers local on tie
46	      merged.push((l.modifiedAt ?? 0) >= (r.modifiedAt ?? 0) ? l : r);
47	    } else if (!l && b) {
48	      // local-delete: keep remote only if remote was edited after base (edit-beats-delete)
49	      if (r && (r.modifiedAt ?? 0) > (b.modifiedAt ?? 0)) {
50	        merged.push(r);
51	      }
52	      // otherwise drop (local delete wins)
53	    } else if (!r && b) {
54	      // remote-delete: local version wins
55	      if (l) {
56	        merged.push(l);
57	      }
58	    } else if (l && r) {
59	      // modified-in-both (b may or may not exist): last-write-wins, >= prefers local on tie
60	      merged.push((l.modifiedAt ?? 0) >= (r.modifiedAt ?? 0) ? l : r);
61	    }
62	  }
63	
64	  return merged;
65	}
66

The merge logic is the heart of it. I can already see the suspicious branch — **edit-beats-delete** (lines 47-52): a locally-deleted child gets *resurrected* if `remote.modifiedAt > base.modifiedAt`. For a file that was just **replaced** (modifiedAt bumped to v2's time), if the `base` passed to the merge is stale (pre-replace), this branch re-adds the file we just deleted. Let me confirm by reading how merge is invoked — what's passed as base/local/remote — and the deleteToBin path.

180	 *
181	 * Encrypts the metadata with the folder key, uploads to IPFS,
182	 * and publishes an updated IPNS record pointing to the new CID.
183	 *
184	 * @returns New CID and sequence number
185	 */
186	export async function updateFolderMetadataAndPublish(params: {
187	  children: FolderChild[];
188	  baseChildren?: FolderChild[];
189	  folderKey: Uint8Array;
190	  ipnsPrivateKey: Uint8Array;
191	  ipnsPublicKey?: Uint8Array;
192	  ipnsName: string;
193	  sequenceNumber: bigint;
194	  ctx: SdkContext;
195	  encryptedIpnsPrivateKey?: string;
196	  keyEpoch?: number;
197	}): Promise<{ cid: string; newSequenceNumber: bigint; publishedChildren: FolderChild[] }> {
198	  return withPerf('folder:update-publish', async () => {
199	    // Merge-and-republish retry loop (D-01 through D-05).
200	    // encrypt+upload happens inside the loop so each attempt gets a fresh CID (D-03).
201	    let currentSeq = params.sequenceNumber;
202	    let currentLocalChildren: FolderChild[] = params.children;
203	    let lastRemoteSeq: bigint = params.sequenceNumber;
204	
205	    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 4; attempt++) {
206	      // 1. Build v2 metadata with current children, encrypt, upload → fresh CID (D-03)
207	      const metadata: FolderMetadata = {
208	        version: 'v2',
209	        children: currentLocalChildren,
210	      };
211	      const encrypted = await encryptFolderMetadata(metadata, params.folderKey);
212	      const jsonStr = JSON.stringify(encrypted);
213	      const encryptedBytes = new TextEncoder().encode(jsonStr);
214	      const { cid } = await addToIpfs(params.ctx, encryptedBytes);
215	
216	      // 2. CAS publish: expectedSequenceNumber guards against lost update
217	      const newSeq = currentSeq + 1n;
218	      try {
219	        await createAndPublishIpnsRecord({
220	          ipnsPrivateKey: params.ipnsPrivateKey,
221	          ipnsPublicKey: params.ipnsPublicKey,
222	          ipnsName: params.ipnsName,
223	          metadataCid: cid,
224	          sequenceNumber: newSeq,
225	          encryptedIpnsPrivateKey: params.encryptedIpnsPrivateKey,
226	          keyEpoch: params.keyEpoch,
227	          expectedSequenceNumber: currentSeq.toString(),
228	          ctx: params.ctx,
229	        });
230	        return { cid, newSequenceNumber: newSeq, publishedChildren: currentLocalChildren };
231	      } catch (err) {
232	        if (!is409(err)) throw err;
233	
234	        // 3. Re-resolve authoritatively — ignore any seq hint in the error body (Pitfall 1+2)
235	        const resolved = await resolveIpnsRecord(params.ipnsName, params.ctx);
236	        if (!resolved) {
237	          throw new ConflictError(params.ipnsName, attempt + 1, lastRemoteSeq);
238	        }
239	        currentSeq = resolved.sequenceNumber;
240	        lastRemoteSeq = resolved.sequenceNumber;
241	
242	        // 4. Re-fetch + decrypt remote folder metadata
243	        const remote = await fetchAndDecryptMetadata(resolved.cid, params.folderKey, params.ctx);
244	
245	        // 5. Three-way merge (D-01 / D-02)
246	        if (params.baseChildren !== undefined) {
247	          // D-01: caller provided base snapshot → proper three-way merge
248	          currentLocalChildren = mergeChildren(
249	            params.baseChildren,
250	            currentLocalChildren,
251	            remote.children
252	          );
253	        } else {
254	          // D-02: no base → union fallback; log warning so the caller sweep is noticeable
255	          console.warn(
256	            '[sdk-core] updateFolderMetadataAndPublish: baseChildren not provided for ' +
257	              params.ipnsName +
258	              ' — using union fallback (deletes may resurrect). Caller should pass baseChildren.'
259	          );
260	          currentLocalChildren = mergeChildren([], currentLocalChildren, remote.children);
261	        }
262	
263	        // 6. After the final attempt, throw ConflictError (D-05)
264	        if (attempt === 3) {
265	          throw new ConflictError(params.ipnsName, 4, lastRemoteSeq);
266	        }
267	
268	        // 7. Backoff + jitter before next attempt (D-04)
269	        await new Promise<void>((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, retryDelayMs(attempt)));
270	      }
271	    }
272	
273	    // Unreachable fallback (TypeScript exhaustion — ConflictError thrown inside the loop above)
274	    throw new ConflictError(params.ipnsName, 4, lastRemoteSeq);
275	  });
276	}

Good — the delete paths *do* pass `baseChildren` (e.g. `bin/index.ts:236`, `useFileOperations.ts:460`), so the D-02 union fallback isn't it. That points to the **edit-beats-delete resurrection** (`merge.ts:47-52`): it fires only when `remote.modifiedAt > base.modifiedAt` for the deleted child — i.e. when the `base` snapshot is stale relative to remote. TC08 is the one test that bumps a child's `modifiedAt` (the replace) right before deleting. Let me read the actual delete + replace paths to find where the stale base comes from.

224	export async function addToBin(params: {
225	  folderIpnsName: string;

231	}): Promise<{ removedItem: FolderChild; updatedBinState: BinState }> {
232	  const folder = params.folderTree.get(params.folderIpnsName);
233	  if (!folder) throw new Error('Folder not loaded');
234	
235	  // 1. Remove item from folder metadata
236	  const baseChildren = [...folder.children];
237	  const { updatedChildren, removedItem } = sdkCore.deleteFromFolder({
238	    children: folder.children,
239	    childId: params.childId,
240	  });
241	
242	  // 2. Publish updated folder metadata
243	  const { newSequenceNumber, publishedChildren } = await sdkCore.updateFolderMetadataAndPublish({
244	    children: updatedChildren,
245	    baseChildren,
246	    folderKey: folder.folderKey,
247	    ipnsPrivateKey: folder.ipnsKeypair.privateKey,
248	    ipnsName: params.folderIpnsName,
249	    sequenceNumber: folder.sequenceNumber,
250	    ctx: params.binCtx.ctx,
251	  });
252	
253	  // 3. Update folder state — adopt merged published set (CR-01)
254	  folder.children = publishedChildren;
255	  folder.sequenceNumber = newSequenceNumber;
256	  folder.lastLoadedAt = Date.now();
257	  params.folderTree.set(params.folderIpnsName, folder);
258	
259	  // 4. Build bin entry
260	  const isFile = removedItem.type === 'file';
261	  const entry: BinEntry = {
262	    id: crypto.randomUUID(),
263	    itemType: isFile ? 'file' : 'folder',
264	    name: removedItem.name,
265	    originalParentIpnsName: params.folderIpnsName,
266	    originalPath: params.parentPath,
267	    deletedAt: Date.now(),
268	    size: 0,
269	    mimeType: '',
270	    filePointer: isFile ? (removedItem as FilePointer) : undefined,
271	    folderEntry: !isFile ? (removedItem as FolderEntry) : undefined,
272	  };
273

287	    entries: updatedEntries,
288	    sequenceNumber: newBinSeq,
289	    ipnsName: params.binState.ipnsName,
290	  };
291	
292	  return { removedItem, updatedBinState };
293	}

`addToBin` reads base from `folder.children` in the SDK's `folderTree` (line 236), and adopts `publishedChildren` after (line 254). So the SDK folderTree stays consistent *within* SDK operations. The question is whether the **replace** updated that same store. The replace goes through `useFileVersions.ts` calling `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` directly — possibly a *different* state store than the delete reads. > AGENT

This is the version-restore path, but it reveals a key architectural fact: `replaceFileInFolder` publishes the **file's** IPNS record and leaves **folder metadata untouched**, while bumping `modifiedAt` only in the local web store (lines 113-126). That creates a local-vs-remote `modifiedAt` skew — exactly the precondition for the merge's edit-beats-delete branch. Before I over-theorize on direction, let me get ground truth from the Playwright trace, which captures the browser console (ConflictError? resurrection? hung promise?).

**Found it.** `useFileOperations.ts:458-480` — after a replace, the folder-metadata republish (step "6b") is **fire-and-forget (not awaited)**. It publishes the bumped `modifiedAt` at `parentFolder.sequenceNumber` in the background and only then updates the store's sequence. The replace returns before that lands. Now I need to confirm the delete reads a *stale* sequence/base (which produces both the 409 and the `remote.modifiedAt > base.modifiedAt` resurrection). Let me verify how the web soft-delete bridges to the SDK and whether it shares sequence state with that background publish.

40	/**
41	 * Build a breadcrumb-style path string for a folder by walking up the tree.
42	 * e.g., "My Vault / Documents / Reports"
43	 */
44	function buildFolderPath(folderId: string): string {
45	  const folders = useFolderStore.getState().folders;
46	  const parts: string[] = [];
47	  let currentId: string | null = folderId;
48	
49	  while (currentId !== null) {
50	    const folder: FolderNode | undefined = folders[currentId];
51	    if (!folder) break;
52	    // Stop before including the root node (its name is already the "My Vault" prefix)
53	    if (folder.parentId === null) break;
54	    parts.unshift(folder.name);
55	    currentId = folder.parentId;
56	  }
57	
58	  return parts.length > 0 ? `My Vault / ${parts.join(' / ')}` : 'My Vault';
59	}

=== ensureFolderRegistered ===
94:export function ensureFolderRegistered(folder: FolderNode): void {
107:  client.registerFolder(
---
export function ensureFolderRegistered(folder: FolderNode): void {
  const client = getSdkClient();

  // Don't overwrite if SDK already has this folder — its internal state
  // is authoritative (correct sequence number, keys, children from mutations)
  if (client.hasFolder(folder.ipnsName)) return;

  // Guard: don't register placeholder folders with empty key material —
  // would cause crypto/IPNS errors on subsequent mutations
  if (folder.folderKey.length === 0 || folder.ipnsPrivateKey.length === 0) {
    return;
  }

  client.registerFolder(
    folder.ipnsName,
    folder.folderKey,
    {
      publicKey: new Uint8Array(0), // Public key derived from private key when needed
      privateKey: folder.ipnsPrivateKey,
    },
    folder.children,
    folder.sequenceNumber
  );
}
```

> AGENT

**Root cause confirmed.** `ensureFolderRegistered` (sdk-provider.ts:94) **no-ops if the folder is already registered** — "SDK internal state is authoritative ... never re-syncs." But the **replace path bypasses the SDK client entirely**: `updateFile` calls sdk-core's `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` directly and updates only the web `useFolderStore` (seq → S+1, `modifiedAt` → t2). The SDK client's `folderTree` is never told, so it stays at the **old seq S and old `modifiedAt` t1**.

Then `deleteToBin` reads that stale `folderTree`:
- publishes removal at stale seq S → remote is already at S+1 → **409** ✓ (the single 409 in the trace)
- merges with stale `baseChildren` (X@t1) against remote (X@t2) → edit-beats-delete sees `remote.modifiedAt(t2) > base.modifiedAt(t1)` → **resurrects the file** ✓ ("Synced", item persists)

Let me confirm the delete invocation and whether `ensureFolderRegistered` is even called on this path.

Confirmed: `handleDelete` calls `ensureFolderRegistered` before `deleteToBin`, but it no-ops on the already-registered root folder — so the stale `folderTree` is used. That's the precise defect.

The fix belongs at the desync point: the SDK `folderTree` must reconcile when the web store has advanced past it (via the replace's direct publish). Let me read `registerFolder` and the folder-tree state shape to design a clean sync.

`registerFolder` fully rebuilds the entry (and would reset the public key to empty), so I'll add a focused in-place reconcile method instead. The fix: when a folder is already tracked, adopt the web store's state **only if its IPNS sequence is strictly newer** — which is exactly the replace case (web store at S+1, folderTree at S), while preserving the "SDK authoritative" invariant when the folderTree is ahead.

=== getRootFolderState ===
export function getRootFolderState(
  vaultStore: ReturnType<typeof useVaultStore.getState>,
  folders: Record<string, FolderNode>
): FolderNode | null {
  if (!vaultStore.rootFolderKey || !vaultStore.rootIpnsKeypair || !vaultStore.rootIpnsName) {
    return null;
  }

  // If we have an explicit root folder in the tree, use it
  const existingRoot = folders['root'];
  if (existingRoot) return existingRoot;

  // Otherwise construct from vault state
  return {
    id: 'root',
    name: 'My Vault',
    ipnsName: vaultStore.rootIpnsName,
    parentId: null,
    children: [],
    isLoaded: false,
    isLoading: false,
    sequenceNumber: 0n,
    folderKey: vaultStore.rootFolderKey,
    ipnsPrivateKey: vaultStore.rootIpnsKeypair.privateKey,
  };
}

  "old_string": "    // Defensive copy so destroy() -> folderTree.clear() doesn't zero caller buffers\n    this.folderTree.set(ipnsName, {\n      ipnsName,\n      folderKey: new Uint8Array(folderKey),\n      ipnsKeypair: {\n        publicKey: new Uint8Array(ipnsKeypair.publicKey),\n        privateKey: new Uint8Array(ipnsKeypair.privateKey),\n      },\n      sequenceNumber,\n      children,\n      metadata: null,\n      lastLoadedAt: Date.now(),\n    });\n  }\n\n  // ---- Internal state access (for bin/share modules) ----",
  "new_string": "    // Defensive copy so destroy() -> folderTree.clear() doesn't zero caller buffers\n    this.folderTree.set(ipnsName, {\n      ipnsName,\n      folderKey: new Uint8Array(folderKey),\n      ipnsKeypair: {\n        publicKey: new Uint8Array(ipnsKeypair.publicKey),\n        privateKey: new Uint8Array(ipnsKeypair.privateKey),\n      },\n      sequenceNumber,\n      children,\n      metadata: null,\n      lastLoadedAt: Date.now(),\n    });\n  }\n\n  /**\n   * Reconcile an already-registered folder's children + sequence with a newer\n   * snapshot observed outside this client.\n   *\n   * The web app publishes folder metadata directly via sdk-core for some\n   * operations (notably file replace / version edits, which bump `modifiedAt`\n   * and the IPNS sequence in the Zustand store without routing through this\n   * client). That advances the store past the SDK's `folderTree`, which\n   * `registerFolder` otherwise treats as authoritative. A later SDK-routed\n   * mutation (delete, move) would then publish with a stale sequence number\n   * (→ 409) against a stale base snapshot; the 409 merge resolves that by\n   * *resurrecting* the just-deleted child, because edit-beats-delete sees\n   * `remote.modifiedAt > staleBase.modifiedAt`.\n   *\n   * Adopt the snapshot only when it is strictly newer (higher IPNS sequence)\n   * than what the SDK holds, so SDK-routed mutations that have already advanced\n   * the folderTree remain authoritative. Keys, folderKey and metadata are\n   * preserved — only children and sequence move forward. No-op when the folder\n   * is not tracked.\n   *\n   * @param ipnsName - Folder's IPNS name\n   * @param children - Current folder children observed by the caller\n   * @param sequenceNumber - IPNS sequence the caller has observed\n   */\n  reconcileFolderState(\n    ipnsName: string,\n    children: FolderChild[],\n    sequenceNumber: bigint\n  ): void {\n    const existing = this.folderTree.get(ipnsName);\n    if (!existing) return;\n    if (sequenceNumber <= existing.sequenceNumber) return;\n\n    existing.children = [...children];\n    existing.sequenceNumber = sequenceNumber;\n    existing.lastLoadedAt = Date.now();\n    this.folderTree.set(ipnsName, existing);\n  }\n\n  // ---- Internal state access (for bin/share modules) ----"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "    it('registerFolder adds to folder tree', () => {\n      client.registerFolder(\n        'new-ipns',\n        new Uint8Array(32),\n        { publicKey: new Uint8Array(32), privateKey: new Uint8Array(64) },\n        [],\n        0n\n      );\n      expect(client.hasFolder('new-ipns')).toBe(true);\n    });",
  "new_string": "    it('registerFolder adds to folder tree', () => {\n      client.registerFolder(\n        'new-ipns',\n        new Uint8Array(32),\n        { publicKey: new Uint8Array(32), privateKey: new Uint8Array(64) },\n        [],\n        0n\n      );\n      expect(client.hasFolder('new-ipns')).toBe(true);\n    });\n\n    describe('reconcileFolderState', () => {\n      const childV1 = {\n        type: 'file' as const,\n        id: 'file1',\n        name: 'f.txt',\n        fileMetaIpnsName: 'k51file',\n        ipnsPrivateKeyEncrypted: 'abc',\n        createdAt: 0,\n        modifiedAt: 100,\n      };\n      const childV2 = { ...childV1, modifiedAt: 200 };\n\n      function registerAt(seq: bigint) {\n        client.registerFolder(\n          'rec-ipns',\n          new Uint8Array(32).fill(0xaa),\n          { publicKey: new Uint8Array(32), privateKey: new Uint8Array(64).fill(0xbb) },\n          [childV1],\n          seq\n        );\n      }\n\n      it('adopts children + sequence when the snapshot is strictly newer', () => {\n        registerAt(5n);\n        // Simulates a direct file-replace publish having advanced the store to seq 6.\n        client.reconcileFolderState('rec-ipns', [childV2], 6n);\n\n        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get('rec-ipns');\n        expect(folder?.sequenceNumber).toBe(6n);\n        expect(folder?.children).toEqual([childV2]);\n        // Keys are preserved across reconciliation.\n        expect(folder?.ipnsKeypair.privateKey.every((b) => b === 0xbb)).toBe(true);\n      });\n\n      it('ignores a snapshot at an equal or older sequence (SDK state stays authoritative)', () => {\n        registerAt(5n);\n        client.reconcileFolderState('rec-ipns', [childV2], 5n); // equal\n        client.reconcileFolderState('rec-ipns', [childV2], 4n); // older\n\n        const folder = client.getFolderTree().get('rec-ipns');\n        expect(folder?.sequenceNumber).toBe(5n);\n        expect(folder?.children).toEqual([childV1]);\n      });\n\n      it('no-ops when the folder is not registered', () => {\n        expect(() => client.reconcileFolderState('absent-ipns', [childV2], 9n)).not.toThrow();\n        expect(client.hasFolder('absent-ipns')).toBe(false);\n      });\n\n      it('copies the children array so later caller mutation does not leak in', () => {\n        registerAt(1n);\n        const live = [childV2];\n        client.reconcileFolderState('rec-ipns', live, 2n);\n        live.push({ ...childV1, id: 'file2' });\n\n        expect(client.getFolderTree().get('rec-ipns')?.children).toEqual([childV2]);\n      });\n    });"
}

> AGENT

Re-run **failed on the exact same TC08** (179 passed, 1 failed) — so this reproduces **deterministically** on this commit, not a low-probability flake. That confirms the diagnosis and that the fix is warranted. Here's the wrap-up.

## Root cause (confirmed)

A **two-store desync** exposed by #488's new 409-merge:

- The web app keeps folder state in **two** places: the Zustand `useFolderStore` and the SDK client's internal `folderTree`.
- `ensureFolderRegistered` treats the `folderTree` as authoritative and **no-ops once a folder is registered** (`sdk-provider.ts:99`).
- But a **file replace** (`updateFile`) publishes folder metadata **directly via sdk-core**, bumping `modifiedAt` and the IPNS sequence in the *Zustand store only* (`useFileOperations.ts:438-480`, fire-and-forget). The SDK `folderTree` is never told — it stays at the **old sequence and old `modifiedAt`**.
- The subsequent soft-delete routes through `client.deleteToBin` → reads the **stale `folderTree`**, so it:
  1. publishes the removal at a stale sequence → **409** (the single 409 in the trace), and
  2. merges with a stale `baseChildren` (`modifiedAt = t1`) against the remote (`modifiedAt = t2`). The merge's **edit-beats-delete** branch (`merge.ts:49`) sees `remote.modifiedAt > base.modifiedAt` and **resurrects the just-deleted file**.

Net effect: delete "succeeds" (app shows *Synced*), but the file stays in the list — and is also added to the bin (double-presence). The Playwright snapshot corroborated it: the resurrected row showed the **pre-replace v1 size**.

The merge itself is correct given its inputs — the inputs (stale base/sequence) are the defect. So the fix is at the desync, not the merge.

## The fix (3 files)

- **`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`** — new `reconcileFolderState(ipnsName, children, sequenceNumber)`: updates an already-tracked folder's children + sequence **only when the snapshot is strictly newer** (higher IPNS sequence), preserving keys. SDK-advanced state stays authoritative; store-advanced state (from a direct replace publish) is adopted.
- **`apps/web/src/lib/sdk-provider.ts`** — `ensureFolderRegistered` now calls `reconcileFolderState` instead of blindly no-opping when the folder is already registered.
- **`packages/sdk/src/__tests__/client-extended.test.ts`** — 4 unit tests (adopts-newer, ignores equal/older, no-op-when-absent, copies-array).

With this, the delete reads `[X@t2]` at seq `S+1` → CAS matches remote → **no 409, no merge, file removed**. It also degrades safely: if the background replace-publish fails, no 409 occurs at all.

## Verification

- New reconcile tests: **4/4 pass**; full `client-extended.test.ts`: **30/30**.
- Web `tsc --noEmit`: **clean**. ESLint on all 3 files: **clean**. SDK dist rebuilt.
- The 3 failing SDK tests in the broad run are pre-existing `integration.test.ts (live API)` ECONNREFUSED — fail on baseline too, unrelated.

I did **not** commit — `main` is protected and this isn't yet validated end-to-end. The cleanest E2E proof is to put this on a branch and let CI run web-e2e (which now fails TC08 deterministically, so a green run is a definitive confirmation).

name: CI E2E Tests

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  detect-changes:
    name: Detect Changes
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    outputs:
      web: ${{ steps.manual.outputs.web || steps.filter.outputs.web }}
      desktop: ${{ steps.manual.outputs.desktop || steps.filter.outputs.desktop }}
    steps:
      - name: Force all changes on manual dispatch
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'
        id: manual
        run: |
          echo "web=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
          echo "desktop=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"

        if: github.event_name != 'workflow_dispatch'
        with:

      - uses: dorny/paths-filter@v4
        if: github.event_name != 'workflow_dispatch'

  "content": "## Summary\n\nFixes the deterministic web-e2e failure in **recycle-bin `TC08: permanent delete of versioned file reclaims all quota`** (reproduced on both runs of `1abceb4b8`). The merge's **edit-beats-delete** branch (`folder/merge.ts`) sees `remote.modifiedAt > base.modifiedAt` and **resurrects the just-deleted file**.\n\nThe merge is correct given its inputs; the inputs (stale base/sequence) are the defect — so the fix is at the desync, not the merge.\n\n## Fix\n\n- **`packages/sdk/src/client.ts`** — new `reconcileFolderState(ipnsName, children, sequenceNumber)`: advances an already-tracked folder's children + sequence **only when strictly newer** (higher IPNS sequence), preserving keys. SDK-routed mutations stay authoritative.\n- **`apps/web/src/lib/sdk-provider.ts`** — `ensureFolderRegistered` reconciles the `folderTree` forward to the store's state instead of no-opping when already registered.\n\nWith current state, the delete publishes at the correct sequence → no 409, no merge, file removed. Degrades safely: if the background replace-publish fails, the delete sequence still matches remote and no conflict arises.\n\n## Tests\n\n- `packages/sdk/src/__tests__/client-extended.test.ts` — 4 unit tests for `reconcileFolderState` (adopts-newer, ignores equal/older, no-op-when-absent, copies-array). Full file: 30/30 pass.\n- Web `tsc --noEmit` clean; ESLint clean on all changed files.\n\n## Validation\n\nA `web-e2e` run is dispatched against this branch — since TC08 fails deterministically on the base commit, a green run is a definitive confirmation.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n"
}

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/myankelev/.claude/projects/-Users-myankelev-Code-random-cipher-box/memory/project-web-sdk-folder-state-desync.md",
  "content": "---\nname: project-web-sdk-folder-state-desync\ndescription: Web app keeps folder state in TWO stores (Zustand useFolderStore + SDK client folderTree) that can silently drift\nmetadata:\n  type: project\n---\n\nThe web app holds folder state in **two** places that can drift:\n\n1. Zustand `useFolderStore` (apps/web) — drives the UI.\n2. The SDK client's internal `folderTree` (packages/sdk `client.ts`) — used by SDK-routed mutations (`deleteToBin`, `move`, `rename`, folder create).\n\n`ensureFolderRegistered` (apps/web/src/lib/sdk-provider.ts) treats the `folderTree` as authoritative and historically **no-opped once a folder was registered**. But some web ops — notably **file replace / version edits** (`useFileOperations.updateFile`, `useFileVersions`) — publish folder metadata **directly via sdk-core** (`updateFolderMetadataAndPublish`) and update only the Zustand store, bumping its IPNS `sequenceNumber` + child `modifiedAt`. The SDK `folderTree` is never told → it goes stale.\n\n**Why it bit (PR #489, web-e2e recycle-bin TC08):** after a replace, a soft-delete via the stale `folderTree` published at a stale sequence → 409, then the new 409 merge (`folder/merge.ts`) merged against a stale base whose `modifiedAt` predated remote → **edit-beats-delete resurrected the just-deleted file**. Deterministic, not flaky.\n\n**Why:** two sources of truth for the same IPNS sequence with no sync at the boundary; the 409-merge made the desync visible by \"resolving\" the conflict wrongly.\n\n**How to apply:** when touching folder mutations or the merge, treat **IPNS `sequenceNumber` as the version clock**. The fix added `client.reconcileFolderState(ipnsName, children, seq)` (adopts a snapshot only when strictly newer) and made `ensureFolderRegistered` call it instead of no-opping. If you add a new web path that publishes folder metadata directly via sdk-core, make sure the SDK `folderTree` gets reconciled before the next SDK-routed mutation. Related: [[project-cross-package-dist-staleness]] (rebuild sdk dist after the new public method).\n"
}
```

7	## 2026-05-31: GSD execute-phase 03.1 (Airtable → Postgres/TypeORM migration, irl-network)
8	
9	### Project Context
10	- **Repo:** irl-network
11	- **Task:** Wave-based GSD phase execution — 5 strictly-linear plans (deps → entities/migration → repos → cutover → Testcontainers harness)
12	
13	### Key Learnings
14	
15	#### 1. Disable git-worktree isolation for LINEAR phases that install deps mid-phase
16	**Problem:** GSD `execute-phase` defaults to `isolation="worktree"` per plan when `workflow.use_worktrees=true`. But here Plan 01 ran `pnpm add typeorm pg`, and Plans 02–05 *use* those deps (migrations, Testcontainers, repos). Git worktrees do NOT share `node_modules` (it's gitignored), so isolating later plans would strand them without their dependencies.
17	
18	**Lesson:** Worktree isolation only pays off for *parallel* plans with disjoint files. For a strictly-linear dependency chain (one plan per wave, each depends on all prior), it provides zero parallelism and actively breaks when an early plan installs deps a later plan needs. Run executors sequentially on the main tree instead; each executor then owns its own STATE.md/ROADMAP.md updates.
19	
20	**Action:** Before dispatching, check the wave map. If every wave is a single plan with a linear `depends_on` chain — or any early plan modifies `package.json`/lockfile that later plans rely on — spawn executors WITHOUT `isolation="worktree"` (sequential main-tree mode).

The merge wasn't wrong — its inputs were. Fix the desync, not the merge.\n**Action:** When two stores mirror the same IPNS-backed entity, reconcile by **strictly-newer sequence number** at the SDK-mutation boundary (added `client.reconcileFolderState`). > AGENT

Everything's complete and validated. Final status:

## ✅ Fixed and validated

| | |
|---|---|
| **Root cause** | Two-store desync: file-replace advances Zustand `useFolderStore` (seq + `modifiedAt`) but not the SDK client `folderTree`; the stale folderTree made the soft-delete publish at a stale sequence → 409 → merge resurrected the deleted file via edit-beats-delete |
| **Fix** | `client.reconcileFolderState` + `ensureFolderRegistered` reconciles folderTree forward when the store's IPNS sequence is strictly newer |
| **PR** | [#489](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/pull/489) (commit `567b6e094`) |
| **web-e2e on branch** | [run 27507854345](https://github.com/FSM1/cipher-box/actions/runs/27507854345) → **180 passed** (was 179/1). TC08 green. |

The fix is proven: TC08 failed deterministically on the base commit (both runs) and passes on the branch.

I also saved the architectural gotcha (two folder-state stores that drift) to project memory and `LEARNINGS.md` so it's not re-discovered the hard way.

Note: CI on the PR runs the standard required checks, but the full `ci-e2e` suite only auto-runs on push to `main` — the branch validation I ran covers web-e2e specifically. Want me to also dispatch `desktop-e2e` against the branch, or anything else before you merge?

> AGENT

Yes — you've put your finger on the actual architectural gap. My fix is a **reconciliation patch, not a cure**: it keeps the two copies from diverging at the SDK-mutation boundary, but the duplication itself — and the whole *class* of bug it enables — is still there. Any future code path that publishes folder metadata without going through the client can reintroduce it.

## It's a known-transitional gap, not by design

`client.ts`'s own doc says so:

> `registerFolder` … *is a bridge method for gradual SDK adoption — eventually all folder loading should go through `client.loadFolder()`.*

So the SDK *intends* to be the single owner of folder state; the web store predates that and the migration was never finished. Two writers exist today:

- **SDK-routed** mutations (`deleteToBin`, `move`, `rename`, create) → update `folderTree`, then emit `folder:updated` → store. Consistent.
- **Direct sdk-core** publishes that bypass the client and write only the store — the leak:
  - `useFileOperations.updateFile` (file replace) — the fire-and-forget "6b" republish
  - `useFileVersions.handleRestoreVersion` / `handleDeleteVersion` (+ lazy IPNS-key migration)

Those bypass paths are exactly what made `folderTree` go stale.

## Recommendation: unify on the SDK client (not the web store)

The SDK client should be the **single source of truth for folder `children` + `sequenceNumber`**, because:

- **The SDK is also consumed headless** — the desktop FUSE mount uses `client` with no Zustand at all. So `folderTree` isn't web cruft to delete; it's the legitimate model for non-web consumers. That rules out the inverse (making the SDK stateless and letting the store own state) — it'd break FUSE.
- Therefore the **web store should become a projection**: it keeps UI/navigation state (`isLoaded`, `currentFolderId`, `breadcrumbs`, the `parentId` tree) and a *render copy* of children, but its `children`/`sequenceNumber` are written **only** by `folder:updated` events — never by web mutation code.

Concretely, the gap closes when the bypass paths become client methods (e.g. `client.replaceFile()` / `client.updateFileMetadata()`) that own the publish + sequence bookkeeping + event emission internally. Then `reconcileFolderState` becomes dead code you can delete — the desync is impossible *by construction* rather than patched by convention.

## Honest caveats on what I shipped

- It fixes the deterministic case (read-stale-then-mutate) but a sub-second race remains: a delete firing between the replace's publish landing and its `.then` updating the store seq. TC08's 3s wait hides it; it's narrow but real.
- It's reconcile-by-convention — the next person adding a direct-publish path won't know to keep the store seq correct.

So: real gap, worth closing, and the SDK is the right place. It's a medium, multi-PR refactor though — not something to fold into this bugfix PR (which should stay a tight, reviewable fix).

Want me to **file a tracking issue** (or add it to the `docs/` backlog) capturing this — the bypass-path inventory, the projection design, and "delete `reconcileFolderState` once done" as the exit criterion? I can draft it without touching the refactor itself.

They drift because some web paths publish folder metadata directly via sdk-core, bypassing the client and updating only the store. This caused PR #489's deterministic web-e2e failure (recycle-bin TC08): a file replace advanced the store's sequence + child modifiedAt but left folderTree stale; the next soft-delete published at a stale sequence → 409 → the 409 merge's edit-beats-delete resurrected the just-deleted file. PR #489 shipped a reconciliation patch (client.reconcileFolderState + ensureFolderRegistered) — a band-aid, not a cure.\n\nBypass-path inventory (the leak): useFileOperations.updateFile (file replace, the fire-and-forget \"6b\" republish); useFileVersions.handleRestoreVersion and handleDeleteVersion (+ lazy IPNS-key migration). These call sdk-core updateFolderMetadataAndPublish/replaceFileInFolder directly and update only the Zustand store.\n\nProposed design: Make the SDK client the single source of truth for folder children + sequenceNumber. The SDK is also consumed headless (desktop FUSE mount uses client with no Zustand), so the SDK — not the store — must own state; making the SDK stateless is ruled out. Convert the bypass paths into client methods (e.g. client.replaceFile() / client.updateFileMetadata()) that own publish + sequence bookkeeping + folder:updated emission. The web useFolderStore becomes a projection: keeps UI/navigation state (isLoaded, currentFolderId, breadcrumbs, parentId tree) and a render copy of children, but children/sequenceNumber are written only via folder:updated events, never by web mutation code. Aligns with the existing \"gradual SDK adoption\" bridge comment on client.registerFolder.\n\nExit criteria: delete client.reconcileFolderState (becomes dead code — desync impossible by construction). Also closes the residual sub-second race in PR #489 (delete firing between a replace's publish landing and its .then updating the store seq).\n\nScope: medium, multi-PR refactor. 10	
11	## Problem
12	
13	Phase 44 added two independent 409-conflict retry engines that duplicate the same
14	skeleton (resolve -> encrypt+upload -> CAS publish -> detect 409 -> re-resolve ->
15	re-fetch+decrypt remote -> domain merge -> retry/backoff -> ConflictError):
16	
17	- `updateFolderMetadataAndPublish` (folder/index.ts): a 4-attempt `for` loop with
18	  exponential backoff + jitter, merging via `mergeChildren`.
19	- `updateFileMetadata` (file/index.ts): a hand-unrolled 2-attempt try/catch with
20	  no backoff, merging via `mergeVersions` + loser-as-version.
21	
22	They have already drifted (4 vs 2 attempts; backoff vs none). A future change to
23	retry count, backoff, or sequence handling must be made twice and is easy to apply
24	to only one path.
25	
26	Surfaced by `/simplify` (2026-06-14). The smaller duplications it found were already
27	fixed in that pass: the inline 409 predicate was deduped into `is409`, and the
28	file-side fetch+decode+decrypt into `fetchAndDecryptFileMetadata`. This larger
29	unification was deferred as out of scope for a quality-only pass.
30	
31	## Solution
32	
33	TBD — key considerations:
34	
35	- Extract a generic `publishWithCas` in sdk-core that owns the
36	  resolve -> encrypt -> upload -> CAS -> 409-classify -> re-resolve -> re-fetch ->
37	  retry skeleton and throws `ConflictError` on exhaustion.
38	- Folder supplies `mergeChildren`; file supplies `mergeVersions` + loser-as-version
39	  as the domain merge callback.
40	- Reconcile the attempt-count/backoff divergence intentionally (pick the right
41	  values for both) rather than preserving the accidental difference.
42	- Keep the CR-02 `prunedCids` reference-filter behavior for the file path.
43
